ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e2977	Federal Minister! I'm asking for a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e2992	The training reform was for us one of the priorities of the reforming or restructuring of the Austrian army at all. We have therefore put in place decisive measures both in the training of basic defence workers and in the training of sub-offenders and officers. In the case of basic defence servants in the form that we have developed a new training concept, where we have distinguished between basic training and association training. With this, we have made clear requirements for the suitability for use and for the objectives to be achieved and have also created more space. As far as the field of sub-official training is concerned, we have upgraded the army sub-official school to a army sub-official academy. We have introduced new courses, new courses and a new framework guide. In the case of the officers, we have made a further improvement in the form of that from 1. January of next year the military academy in Wiener Neustadt will also have the status of a high school course, and thus this area has also been raised at international level.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e4628	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e4643	A NATO membership, i.e. a full entry into NATO, has no direct impact in terms of obligations or of certain advantages in the procurement sector. This means that one can actually switch to minimum stocks and that, of course, one can also buy significantly cheaper together than if one goes into self-development for a small army as a relatively small country or into the full creation of large-scale systems. Just the same view also represents all the new members of NATO, who are to be taken over as candidates, as Hungary, as I could only learn directly from a conversation last week, or the Czechs or the Poles, but also the old members. And the advantage that can be derived directly from it is that there is a life outside the barracks, that is, cadre staff with groundmen, the whole team goes out and carries out a mission. They can not only test certain organizational and management systems but also have to live them. Furthermore, I have also found that the basic defence servants themselves can draw a very high political profit from this performance, in the form that they are faced for the first time with refugee questions, that they also deal with what fate lies behind it or which fate is behind the attempt to illegally cross a border. There is a great deal of discussion about this, and I see it as a further education in the humanitarian sense or in the sense of democracy policy.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e5336	Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00174/index.shtml -- 174/M]] What concrete measures do you plan in the area of family and divorce law?

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e5379	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen! In general, the aim of the reform is to strengthen the economically weaker partner in marriage and in the case of divorce, in particular in the division of tasks during marriage, in the protection against loss of housing and in the division and maintenance area. Specific measures are planned in matrimonial law, divorce law and divorce law. The aim of the matrimonial law is to clarify the obligation to form a partnership between the marital community, in particular the burden on employment, budget management and childcare to be shared as evenly as possible. The agreement reached is intended to be one-sided for particularly legitimate reasons from a partner, for example when starting a professional activity. – Maintenance also in money instead of just in kind, safeguarding the housing needs, abolishing the participation obligation in the acquisition of the spouse, better remuneration if such participation takes place. In the case of divorce, it is likely that it will not be possible to proceed completely from the principle of indebtedness. However, we want to sum up all the grounds for indebtedness in an indebted state, serious adultery with destruction. In the case of divorce, however, the principle of indebtedness should come into the background, as far as it is possible to take greater account of the need than before, for example by granting the needy maintenance regardless of the indebtedness, if the needyness is so prejudicial, whether it was not able to take up a profession within the marriage because of child care, or if it were after that. The maintenance should also be temporary.

It has a lot to catch up with in Austria, and there are a lot of ideas that need to be realized and that are also in preparation to lift traffic safety in Austria. I think these are signals that we should pay attention to. EU Transport Commissioner Neil Kinnock insisted that lower alcohol limits be introduced in all EU countries, says 0.5 Promille. Most EU countries have complied with this call. Austria is one of the last EU countries to go there. There are only a few EU countries that have not yet done so. The EU has every reason to move forward in its road safety efforts, as there are around 45 000 people in the EU each year who lose their lives on the road. About 10 percent of these are due to alcoholic handlebars. The yes to the introduction of the 0.5-promille limit is also reflected in public opinion. A survey states that two thirds of Austrian citizens are in favour. The reduction of the alcohol limit to 0.1 per mille for starters was also a really good sign, as Mr Peter said. It was widely accepted by young people. What we can do today is – and we owe it to the citizens of our country – to give signs to people, to life, to appeal to drivers and to all other road users, to be aware of their responsibility. We ask the media to support us. Maybe then we will have as another step 0.0 Promille in road traffic. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e9253	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Today we are expected to decide to cut the promille limit to 0.5. This measure is intended to reduce the number of road accidents occurring under the influence of alcohol and drugs. In this context, an own motion for a resolution on improving road safety was tabled in addition to the legislative proposal for a reduction to 0.5 promille in order to create the legal conditions. Ladies and gentlemen! The legislator can only create the conditions for transport safety. However, it is more important to strengthen awareness of the proper conduct of road transport, and I believe that we should also consider our own attitude in today's debate. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Hans Helmut Moser: Why now?]] However, ladies and gentlemen, I would also like to ask the Transport Safety Board to be more active in this area of awareness-building in the future. [[New congratulations to the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! ==References== November is a new driving licence law. This driving licence law regulates for the first time the steering licence for young people aged 15 and over. This is particularly important for young people in rural areas who are very difficult to get to work or school with public transport. The driving licence law now regulates the possibility of the head of state to issue a moped card by means of a regulation, if specific conditions are required. These requirements will now be standardised: Firstly, proof of the applicant's mental maturity is required by an opinion of a traffic-psychological examination body, second, confirmation by the teacher or school that there is no public transport that cannot be carried out or is only available on the basis of the timetable, and thirdly, a declaration of consent of the person entitled to the education. Unfortunately, the health regulation of the Minister of Transport, which regulates the precise modality of the transport psychological examination, made it much more difficult to obtain the moped card. In fact, the driving licence law health regulation makes no difference between applicants for the moped card from 15 years old and those candidates who have already failed to pass the theoretical or practical part of the driving test several times and who, due to a supplementary official medical examination, have doubts about their power-driven performance, especially in intelligence and in the ability to remember. Ladies and gentlemen! It is not justified that a youth whose spiritual maturity is to be determined must carry out the same test as an adult whose intelligence is doubted, since he has already failed to pass the driving licence test several times. In addition to the complicated procedure, the price of 5 000 s for this study is hardly affordable for young people. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! It should be sufficient for the 15-year-olds, as is the case with the applicants for an advanced steering test of Class B, to undergo only 1 800 s of expensive traffic psychological screening. That is why I welcome the Committee on Transport's resolution, which mandates the Federal Minister for Science and Transport to amend the driving licence health regulation accordingly. Mr Federal Minister, I ask you to comply with this request from the Transport Committee. The young people will be grateful to you.

In this context, it is very precise how many tables are to be attached; Exceptions are created to ensure that there are two panels on some vehicles, but only one board on other vehicles. There is even a regulatory authority, according to which the Federal Minister for Science and Transport has to determine exactly what sizes and labels are to be chosen, what color the boards must have, how they are issued, how they are returned, who has to bear the costs of production and administration and how they should be exacted. We believe that this is a classic example of the fact that there are, in fact, provisions which can be left directly to those who ultimately deal with these vehicles. The legislator must not be interested in whether the cost of manufacturing is to be borne by the person who hires a vehicle, or whether that is taken over by the landlord of the vehicle. This is not something that must be regulated in federal laws, it is overwhelming. We believe that this is a sign of too much bureaucracy being created in this context. But the real and decisive reason for our rejection is that all of this has so far been regulated by regulation, although there has only been a directive at European level. Now the legal basis for this is created, and it becomes retroactive with 1. It was put into effect on September 1995. That is to say, one makes a provision which has been put into effect for two years retroactively, only to cover the fact that one actually had for two years a regulation that was not based on any national legal basis. Ladies and gentlemen! This is a way in which the Liberals believe that we should all abstain, because we do not think we should have a retroactive effect on these grounds, because this would increase the inconsistency of the legal regulations in that country even more. That's something the Liberals don't want. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e12361	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! These transport laws – freight transport law, rail law adaptation law, rail infrastructure financing law – are primarily an adaptation to EU directives. We welcome and support these new laws. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The accession of Austria to the European Union also led to the adoption by Austria of the EU Directive on the establishment of common rules for certain combined transport services between Member States. I would just like to say the following: By implementing these Directives in this Act, cross-border combined transport will be promoted by the freedom of authorisation of the advance and follow-up. We expressly welcome this because this should lead to a greater shift in freight transport on the rails and, as we hope, in cross-border transport. The Railway Adjustment Act includes the conditions for access by other foreign and domestic railway undertakings – in addition to the ÖBB – to cross-border rail transport. This will also make possible on the rail network and on the road of competition between individual railways. The basic implementation of an EU directive, which is also in line with the long-standing demands of the Austrian People's Party, is thus also in line with our agreement. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In connection with the Rail Law Adjustment Act and the Rail Infrastructure Finance Act, the pension regulations of the railways have also been amended in a section 27-applicant. This will also implement the pension reform for the ÖBB staff, as the last stage of the major reform project, the harmonisation of the Austrian pension system. I believe that we have found in everything a reasonable and fair arrangement. Very briefly on the priorities: A key point of harmonisation was the uniformisation of the calculation period for the calculation of pensions. The conversion arrangements are introduced by the public authorities of the public sector, as well as by the public authorities. For the suspension of hardness during the transitional period, a lid is also introduced for the ÖBB, which limits the calculation losses. For active officials and pensioners, the pension contribution is gradually increased to 4 percent. In order to provide an incentive for longer work and to increase the average retirement age of 53 years, ÖBB staff will be provided between 53 and 60. Retire in old age, reduced by 0.2 percentage points for each year later in the pension scheme. For ÖBB staff, stricter retirement rules will be applied than for officials. There are no different pension provisions before the 60. Year of life and between 60 and 65. Year of life. The increase in pensions will take place from 2000 on for ÖBB staff in accordance with the same provisions as for the ASVG-pensionists. In the future, the ÖBB will have 30 percent of the Federal Railways' operating expenses to the federal government instead of 26 percent of the effort. ÖBB officials will also have to pay unemployment insurance contributions in the future. Ladies and gentlemen! These are the most important innovations in the pension reform of the Austrian Federal Railways. These new schemes are largely on a legal basis or by a legal reference to the pension regulations negotiated between the Board of Directors of the ÖBB and the trade union, which are essentially based on the individual contracts of the ÖBB staff. In our view, this was necessary in order to achieve such a firmness and legal certainty, without therefore wanting to question the individual contracts of the Federal Railways. The Federal Government also reserves the right to make the pension payment to ÖBB officials only to the extent that corresponds to the implementation of the pension reform. This was also set accordingly by law. In addition, a ministerial statement to the Board of Directors is that the Board of Directors of the ÖBB can change this pension scheme only with the approval of the Supervisory Board and the Annual General Meeting, i.e. the Minister in agreement with the Federal Government. Overall and finally, I can say that an acceptable result has been achieved in the interests of taxpayers and the equal treatment of all workers and pensioners – a compromise to which we confess. There is no doubt that the retirement age of the railways has not been reduced. Certainly, the railways have to pay a higher pension contribution and pensioners a pension contribution of 3 and 4 percent respectively – so to speak, as in return for their earlier retirement age. Ladies and gentlemen! However, this is not really an equal treatment. This has to be determined. In this sense, this issue remains on the agenda for the next stage of pension reform.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e13593	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! In the spring of this year, the very successful genetic engineering people's desire, which was signed by more than 1.2 million people, took place. The three demands of genetic engineering folk desire relate exclusively to genetic applications in the fields of agriculture and nutrition, not to basic research and medical applications. The three claims were as follows: There should be no patent for life, no food from the gene laboratory and no releases of genetically modified plants in Austria. The two first-mentioned claims have already been de facto reversed by the hesitation or consciously by other decisions of the government parties. The European Patenting Directive, which very probably provides for patents on animals and plants and on certain conditions also on human gene sequences, has been approved. Food from the genetic laboratory, provided it has been produced in other countries, seems to be fundamentally secured by the free movement of goods. A national ban on production, which would have been possible, does not want to be political. The last Committee meeting was about the third request, namely the issue of releases in Austria. This question is an extremely urgent one, because, according to the existing genetic engineering law – and Mrs Prammer has done so quite openly in the Committee – she cannot ignore requests for the release of genetically modified plants that companies will submit in the coming spring. It must create complete facts before Parliament has been able to decide on the requirement not to grant releases in Austria. All of this takes place almost daily and weekly of new knowledge published in serious scientific journals, according to which the potential of genetic engineering in agriculture is considerably greater than was initially accepted by the authorities and the applicant companies. We know that the pollen flight of genetically modified plants goes far beyond what was originally assumed. We know that the oozing of genetically modified plants into wild species has already taken place for impossible, and we know from the U.S. where it is currently being debated about a drastic intensification of the law, that the main argument of critics, namely that herbicides and pesticides can be transferred from plants to organisms, has unfortunately already become striking in reality. The first genetic engineering GAUs have already taken place. We do not yet know what extent they have and how much they will influence agricultural production in the future. In this context, the increasingly manifest dangers of natural science, in the light of the false assumptions of the genetic engineering lobby and the errors of authorities, have put minimum requirements for environmental organizations, which should be so obvious in a rule of law that I am really shocked to have to hold a deadline debate here at all. The environmental organisations demand such natural things as party positions for those concerned, such as for a organic farmer whose field lies next to a field where release is to take place and which, possibly – and this would be a banal damage – can no longer sell his harvest as organic products. The experts nominated by the government side have conceded that the regulation of the party position is not included in the law at all, perhaps by analogy could be constructed out of the AVG, but at least it is decided by a supreme court. This means that releases may occur in the coming spring, and the interested parties – not even the immediate neighbours – do not have a secure party position. Even if something happens, there will be no indebted liability with a burden of proof, i.e. ex ante no possibility of participation in administrative proceedings in the context of a party's position, and ex post, if anything has happened, no possibility to claim damages. This is an unicum in the Austrian legal order, something else is nowhere else! Already operators of low-risk plants must, of course, be liable, even holders of animals must be held, but for mega-hazardous genetic engineering one has created a right-free space. In addition, the work in the Committee is still taking place. While we debated in committee and I asked the committee presidents to interrupt the meeting so that we could get a paper to know whether the government parties were at all serious about responding to the minimum demands of the proponents of the popular desire, by the way, that request was not accepted, in the neighbouring area – as we have learned afterwards – there were ministers, Klubreferents, some Members and Dr. Zacherl of the Institute of Molecular Pathology, a person who was even interested in genetic engineering. This small group has drawn up a motion for a resolution which is far behind what Mrs Prammer has already proposed as a legislative proposal, and which the SPÖ had initially announced as a motion for a resolution. Apart from the scientific dangers and the disastrous legal situation in the field of genetic engineering, the result of this committee is a capitulation of violence, a capitulation of parliamentarianism. In response to a law that is clearly inadequate, a resolution is tabled by the government parties – on behalf of the ÖVP and once again with the tolerance of the SPÖ – which reads as follows: The Austrian Federal Government is asked to verify that the existing rights of participation are sufficient for those affected. – In other words, Parliament first decides against the opposition’s recommendations an inadequate law, the government experts confirm this, and now the government groups in Parliament are asking the government to check whether Parliament has built a mess. Ladies and gentlemen! This is really the last thing about Parliament's capitulation. I think that further participation in this committee, also chaired by Mrs Rauch-Kallat, does not make any sense anymore, because there are to be comforted for so long until genetically modified plants grow in the fields until completed facts have been created. That is why, and only then will it be useful to take part in this committee again, will I ask that this Committee have a period of time until the 20th century. ==References==

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e14458	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! The Government template 914 of the Supplements to the Code on the carriage of goods brings some provisions relating to the carriage of goods by road into line with EU law, as well as some provisions which were obsolete by the Commercial Code in 1994. In addition, this proposal also contains some provisions necessary for the introduction of electronic debiting of ecopoints. As you know, ladies and gentlemen, the ecopoints system, which was, by the way, a great success in Austria's accession to the EU in the run-up to accession, was introduced with the aim of reducing the total traffic of goods from transit with regard to nitrogen oxides by 60 percent on the basis of 1991-2003 values. The electronic debiting of ecopoints already planned before the date of introduction of the ecopoints system can now be made with 1. In 1998 the Commission adopted a proposal for a Council Directive on the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and Due to the good planning of the infrastructure conditions for a functioning electronic ecopoint system, it is now possible to integrate electronic devices, so-called environmental data carriers, into the motor vehicles concerned. In the future, these environmental data carriers may record certain information, such as the ecopoints consumed per journey of the vehicle concerned or the origin of the approval. After a certain programming process, the automatic debiting of ecopoints is possible when passing an ecopoints station without stopping. This proposal for a law also allows private companies to carry out this programming process of environmental data carriers, for which, of course, an expense contribution may be lifted. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! However, I would like to say critically about the increasing number of trucks in Austria. Although the ecopoint system implements the reduction of nitrogen oxide emissions, the number of lorries in Austria is increasing. This, at the first moment, is illogical because of the above-average development progress in truck technology. Thus, more and more low nitrogen oxide emissions are produced, but the nitrogen oxide emissions continue to be used as parameters for the ecopoint system. But in addition to nitrogen oxide emissions, there are other negative effects on people and the environment that are generated by truck transport. In particular, air pollutant emissions such as carcinogens and hydrocarbons should be noted. In this respect, the problem of traffic flow is also worth mentioning, which is no longer given to a certain number of trucks on the roads. Essentially in this context is also the increased transport risk caused by the increased number of heavy-duty vehicles in road transport. In order to address this situation, ladies and gentlemen, according to today's state of the art, it seems to me that the only solution is to put freight transit on the environmentally friendly rail. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Similarly, the introduction of an electronic road pricing system as a road cost burden would be an adequate means to achieve a change in the Austrian road landscape. [[New applause at the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! In my opinion, the introduction of the electronic debiting of ecopoints is the technological basis for an Austrian comprehensive electronic road pricing system, which could then ensure cost reliability in transport and ultimately guarantee a reduction in environmental pollution. Finally, I would like to ask you, ladies and gentlemen, to agree in the interests of all this government proposal.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e14927	Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The present postal law represents a final step towards the restructuring of postal services in Austria. ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e14959	The next speaker is Mr Kukacka. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e14981	High house! Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! With the present postal law, the Austrian Post is taking a further step towards liberalisation. A number of these steps have already been taken. The most important was the Poststructural Act: The postal and telegraph management was distributed and converted into a stock company. Now, with this postal law, the PTA also becomes marketable in the "yellow post" sector, so to speak. It offers its services on the free market. That is exactly what we as the Austrian People's Party have always sought, even at those times when we have been severely shocked, and also by our coalition partner. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] We wish for the post as a market-compliant and customer-oriented service company. This postal law brings complete separation of sovereign and operational functions, precisely defines the tasks of the postal authority, but also ensures the maintenance of a comprehensive basic supply in the letter and parcel service. However, if the post does not provide this universal service, other companies can also be entrusted with it. Outside the reserved postal service and universal service, however, the post is clearly in full competition with all other providers. It can move completely freely in this market area. State control rights and approval reservations are not necessary in the light of a functioning market. We welcome this development, and we are convinced that the post has a good future ahead. Of course, this also means a abandonment of the National Council, for example, of the sovereignty of collective bargaining. This will no longer exist in the postal sector, not even by the main committee, but it will continue to be possible to monitor the abuse of the monopoly of universal service by the competent Federal Minister. The postal service is excluded. There are special tariffs for the purchase newspapers and for the notices and information sheets of the non-profit associations. In principle, they do not change the rules of collective bargaining. It will continue to be negotiable in the future in order to achieve a socially compatible and compatible pricing system for non-profit associations. That, ladies and gentlemen, is a right way to do that, as we confess. That is why we have requested that there be a collective bargaining committee, a price commission in which both the representatives of interests and the non-profit associations are represented. This Commission will also provide appropriate advice and support to the Federal Minister for the fixing of tariffs. Ladies and gentlemen! We know that the post, including the Yellow Post, is still facing major challenges. We also know that productivity in this company must be increased. The causes of productivity shortcomings lie precisely in the long-standing monopoly situation of the Austrian Post – a typical consequence of this situation. That is to say, it must be phased down, but socially acceptable, also employees, and I believe that the early retirement scheme, which is advanced and agreed with the employees, as it is sought and implemented, is a right, socially acceptable way. We expect Austrian Post and Telekom AG to be given clear objectives by the Executive Board and the Supervisory Board that this company has a clear customer orientation, that cost awareness in the company is ensured, rationalisation and restructuring are carried out, and that a clear, comprehensive privatisation concept will be presented until the deadline laid down by the law. In this sense, ladies and gentlemen, structural reforms and business policy decisions must be made. And if they are met in this sense, I am not afraid of the future of the Austrian Post. Thank you.

The second part of this request, ladies and gentlemen, refers to the heat pumps. Something that is common in Austria, but many do not know: There are now 130 000 heat pumps in Austria. There are obviously a large number of households and businesses that have been working on how to gain energy in an ecological way since the first oil price shock. Simply put, a heat pump uses a kilowatt electric energy and makes it between three and four kilowatts of energy, depending on the season and performance. All in all, these 130,000 heat pumps use environmental energy according to the OKA 880 gigawatt hours. It is therefore a question of achieving three to four kilowatts of energy with a simple energy use. That is to say, as of 1995 this means a savings of around 1 800 tonnes of SO2 and 850 000 tonnes of CO2. So this is a topic that we have been dealing with right now in Kyoto: How can we save CO2? These heat pumps are, of course, severely restricted by the energy tax on electricity in their cost-effectiveness – for the simple reason that the cost-effectiveness of a heat pump system consists of two functions: One function is the higher investment costs one has in such a high-tech plant, the other is the savings resulting from a lower use of energy, because these plants use environmental energy in relation to 1 : 3 or 1 : 4. I therefore believe, Mr State Secretary - apologise for the disturbance - that it would be useful if one wanted to achieve the new Kyoto target, if one wanted to reduce CO2 emissions, to exempt these modern, forward-looking technologies from an energy tax on electricity, because it is precisely these technologies that enable it to switch from conventional fossil energy sources to modern forms of energy. [[Members speak to State Secretary Dr Ruttenstorfer]] I've allowed myself to submit the request to you, and ask for his examination. If I also know that he is now being rejected, perhaps in the new year he will be able to come back to it very soon and then decide on it. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e18635	Mr President. High house! Let me make a few clarifications on this issue of the credit card company or the pre-tax deduction. Credit card companies provide two types of services, namely, the credit to the card holder – they receive the credit card fee – and on the other hand they take over the credit guarantee and receive a corresponding discount from the entrepreneurs. The credit card companies have so far assumed that the loan to the credit card holder constitutes a turnover-exempted benefit, but that the acquisition of the credit guarantee is a kind of intermediary and therefore taxable. The consequence of this view of the credit card companies which they have already had in the past was that they could benefit from the deduction of pre-tax for the transactions in connection with credit insurance, i.e. for the performance I have mentioned. But this was controversial, and that is why this bill now serves to clarify. We must be clear about this at the 6th. Guide the VAT Directive of the European Union. It is then possible for individual Member States to provide for an option for credit card companies to be subject to VAT, as it should now be. Within the European Union, for example, the Federal Republic of Germany has chosen this system, and we are now going the same way in the course of harmonisation within the European Union and are also making this option possible for Austrian credit card companies. On the question of whether there is a material difference between the variant of granting this possibility only from 1998 onwards, and the variant of granting this possibility retroactively, that is, from 1995 onwards, it should be noted that this retroactive effect does not have any material significance, since, for example, the introduction of this scheme would have made it possible, as you know, for example, to correct it from 1998 onwards, in the course of the pre-tax deduction. That is why we have chosen the clearer regulation of retroactive effect from 1995. On the question of what amount this is, I would like to make it clear that very few companies are affected, namely only four, and that the mention of the amount would therefore allow for conclusions on individual companies. This is therefore not possible in the light of the safeguarding of tax secrecy.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00105_d7e19770	Ladies and gentlemen! We arrived at the end of this sitting. I remember exactly that we were almost exactly a year ago, not on 12. ==References== December, our work has been completed. It was then a very stormy and stressful session. There is now another year behind us in which we have devoted ourselves to many parliamentary issues. We have discussed a lot of templates. We were of differing opinion on many issues, but we were pleased to have assessed a number of them at the same time. Of course, at the end of each year, if possible in the next year you will do so much better or different. It should not be overlooked, however, that in the past year much has been accomplished and can be achieved. This was the case because there has been a common effort by all the members of this House and because we have found support from a considerable number of employees. I think I can say in your name and on my own behalf to the staff of the House and the parliamentary clubs in this place and at this time a warm thanks for their work! [[General condolences.]] I would like to express my special thanks to the address of the Second and Third President of the National Council and to the members of the Presidential Conference, in which we are not always confronted with simple subjects. But I believe there has been virtually no question where we have not found a consensusable way. In most cases, joint recommendations and decisions of the Presidential Conference were found after appropriate efforts and efforts. It is a good habit to send a warm greeting to Austrian soldiers and officers working abroad and to say a word of thanks to them today, and I would like to do so today. [[General condolences.]] We also thank the representatives of the media for fulfilling the task of reporting on parliamentary work, which is certainly not easy. It is clear that without the public, without newspapers, without radio and television, the democratic parliamentary system cannot work. That is why I would also like to extend a warm word of thanks to colleagues from the media and ask them to do so. [[General condolences.]] Finally, I would like to wish you all and your families a beautiful Christmas and an annual change in the sense of your wish, and a successful new year, health and personal wish everything good! [[General condolences.]] The next meeting will take place in January at human discretion. The sitting is closed.
